The midline of the oscillation will be at 69.5 m.\n\nThe wheel takes 30 minutes to complete 1 revolution, so the height will oscillate with a period of 30 minutes.\n\nLastly, because the rider boards at the lowest point, the height will start at the smallest value and increase, following the shape of a vertically reflected cosine curve.\n\nAmplitude: $67.5,$ so $A=67.5$ \nMidline: $69.5,$ so $D=69.5$ \nPeriod: $30,$ so $B=\\frac{2π}{30}=\\frac{π}{15}$ \nShape: $−cos(t)$ \n\nAn equation for the rider’s height would be\n\n$y=-67.5\\cos(\\frac{π}{15}t)+69.5$ \n\nwhere $t$ is in minutes and $y$ is measured in meters."}]]}]]}]

1a2:T51a,This problem should appear familiar as it is similar to a quadratic. Let $\sin θ=x.$ The equation becomes $2x^{2}+x=0.$ We begin by factoring:

$\begin{array}{c}\text{   }2x^{2}+x=0 \\ x(2x+1)=0\end{array}$

Set each factor equal to zero.

$\begin{array}{c}\text{           }x=0\text{  } \\ (2x+1)=0 \\ \text{           }x=-\frac{1}{2}\end{array}$

Then, substitute back into the equation the original expression $\sinθ$ for $x.$ Thus,

$\begin{array}{c}\sin θ=0 \\ \text{     }θ=0,π \\ \\ \sin θ=-\frac{1}{2} \\ \text{     }θ=\frac{7π}{6},\frac{11π}{6}\end{array}$

The solutions within the domain $0\leθ\lt 2π$ are $0,π,\frac{7π}{6},\frac{11π}{6}.$ 

If we prefer not to substitute, we can solve the equation by following the same pattern of factoring and setting each factor equal to zero.

$\begin{array}{c}\text{  }2 \sin^{2}θ+\sin θ=0 \\ \sin θ(2\sin θ+1)=0 \\ \text{                  }\sin θ=0 \\ \text{                       }θ=0,π \\ \\ \text{         }2 \sin θ+1=0 \\ \text{                }2\sin θ=-1 \\ \text{                  }\sin θ=-\frac{1}{2} \\ \text{                       }θ=\frac{7π}{6},\frac{11π}{6}\end{array}$199:["$","li","openstax-precalc-7-5-solving-trigonometric-equations-example-12",{"className":"rounded-md border border-gray-200 1a3:T51a,The velocity at $t=1$ and $t=3$ is the instantaneous rate of change of distance per time, or velocity. Notice that the initial height is 6 feet. To find the instantaneous velocity, we find the derivative and evaluate it at $t=1$ and $t=3:$ 

$\begin{array}{cc}f^{′}(a)=\underline{lim} \frac{f(a+h)-f(a)}{h} & \\ s^{′}(t)=\underline{lim} \frac{-16(t+h)^{2}+64(t+h)+6-(-16t^{2}+64t+6)}{h} & \text{Substitute }s(t+h)\text{ and }s(t). \\ \text{        }=\underline{lim }\frac{-16t^{2}-32ht-h^{2}+64t+64h+6+16t^{2}-64t-6}{h} & \text{Distribute}. \\ \text{        }=\underline{lim} \frac{-32ht-h^{2}+64h}{h} & \text{Simplify}. \\ \text{        }=\underline{lim} \frac{h(-32t-h+64)}{h} & \text{Factor the numerator}. \\ \text{        }=\underline{lim}-32t-h+64 & \text{Cancel out the common factor }h. \\ s^{′}(t)=-32t+64 & \text{Evaluate the limit by letting }h=0.\end{array}$ 

For any value of $t$ , $s^{′}(t)$ tells us the velocity at that value of $t.$ 

Evaluate $t=1$ and $t=3.$ 

$\begin{array}{c}s^{′}(1)=−32(1)+64=32 \\ s^{′}(3)=−32(3)+64=−32\end{array}$

The velocity of the ball after 1 second is 32 feet per second, as it is on the way up.

The velocity of the ball after 3 seconds is $−32$ feet per second, as it is on the way down.19e:["$","details",null,{"className":"mt-1","children":[["$","summary",null,{"className":"text-xs text-purple-600 dark:text-purple-400 cursor-pointer hover:underline","children":"Show solution"}],["$","p",null,{"className":"mt-1 text-xs text-gray-600 dark:text-gray-400 whitespace-pre-wrap leading-relaxed","children":"$1a3"}]]}]
1a4:T53a,If $f(x)=x^{2}-100x$ describes the cost of producing $x$ computers, $f^{′}(x)$ will describe the marginal cost. We need to find the derivative. For purposes of calculating the derivative, we can use the following functions:

$\begin{array}{c}f(x+h)=(x+h)^{2}-100(x+h) \\ \text{      }f(x)=x^{2}-100x\end{array}$

$\begin{array}{cc}\text{    }f^{′}(x)=\underline{lim}\frac{f(x+h)-f(x)}{h} & \text{Formula for a derivative} \\ \text{             }=\underline{lim}\frac{(x+h)^{2}-100(x+h)-(x^{2}-100x)}{h} & \text{Substitute }f(x+h)\text{ and }f(x). \\ \text{             }=\underline{lim}\frac{x^{2}+2xh+h^{2}-100x-100h-x^{2}+100x}{h} & \text{Multiply polynomials, distribute}. \\ \text{             }=\underline{lim}\frac{2xh+h^{2}-100h}{h} & \text{Collect like terms}. \\ \text{             }=\underline{lim}\frac{h(2x+h-100)}{h} & \text{Factor and cancel like terms}. \\ \text{             }=\underline{lim} 2x+h-100 & \text{Simplify}. \\ \text{             }=(2x-100) & \text{Evaluate when }h=0. \\ \text{     }f^{′}(x)=2x-100 & \text{Formula for marginal cost} \\ f^{′}(200)=2(200)-100=300 & \text{Evaluate for 200 units}.\end{array}$ 

The marginal cost of producing the 201 st unit will be approximately \$300.19f:["$","li","openstax-precalc-12-4-derivatives-example-7",{"className":"rounded-md border border-gray-200 Then we evaluate the derivative at $t=2,$ using $s(a+h)=-16(a+h)^{2}+36(a+h)+200$ and $s(a)=-16a^{2}+36a+200.$ \n$\\begin{array}{c}s^{′}(a)=\\underline{lim}\\frac{s(a+h)-s(a)}{h} \\\\ \\text{        }=\\underline{lim}\\frac{-16(a+h)^{2}+36(a+h)+200-(-16a^{2}+36a+200)}{h} \\\\ \\text{        }=\\underline{lim}\\frac{-16(a^{2}+2ah+h^{2})+36(a+h)+200-(-16a^{2}+36a+200)}{h} \\\\ \\text{        }=\\underline{lim}\\frac{-16a^{2}-32ah-16h^{2}+36a+36h+200+16a^{2}-36a-200}{h} \\\\ \\text{        }=\\underline{lim}\\frac{-16a^{2}-32ah-16h^{2}+36a+36h+200+16a^{2}-36a-200}{h} \\\\ \\text{        }=\\underline{lim}\\frac{-32ah-16h^{2}+36h}{h} \\\\ \\text{        }=\\underline{lim}\\frac{h(-32a-16h+36)}{h} \\\\ \\text{        }=\\underline{lim}(-32a-16h+36) \\\\ \\text{        }=-32a-16\\cdot 0+36 \\\\ s^{′}(a)=-32a+36 \\\\ s^{′}(2)=-32(2)+36 \\\\ \\text{        }=-28\\end{array}$"}]]}]]}]
